**Criteria (the contract's, unchanged).** B1 met (tokens identical in every paced run: `g55`, `g57`, `g68`, `g70`, `g76`; `pieces` 27-32 and 108-125 with `pace_wait_us` ~32 ms on every worker-fed bank). B2: the attention clause is met by both configurations at every length (attention-layer `layer_compute` 5.8 / 6.8 / 8.5 ms at 63 / 256 / 1,023 tokens with 16 x 2 and 4.1 / 5.5 / 7.2 with 4 x 3, against 33.2 / 34.1 / 35.9 unpaced); the recurrent clause (within +-2 ms) is met by 4 x 3 everywhere (+1.4 / +1.7 / +1.8 ms) and by 16 x 2 at 256 and 1,023 tokens, and MISSED by 0.09 ms at 63 tokens (+2.09 ms): recorded, not repaired (it is the latency the stall fix is made of; D78). B3 met (16 x 2: -2.8 / -2.2%, -5.4 / -5.3%, -9.5 / -11.9% against the unpaced run at 63 / 256 / 1,023 tokens; 4 x 3: +1.6 / +1.7%, -1.0 / -1.3%, -5.0 / -7.9%). B4 / A4 met on the final build: `g69-ident-*`, three reps, g44 without a new flag against g40, +0.2 / 0.0 / -0.3% (63), -0.1 / +0.3 / +0.3% (256), -0.1 / +0.8 / -0.3% (1,023), tokens identical (the first run, `g57-pace-63`, had a 6% slow control in rep 1, 1,438 against 1,352 ms, which did not reproduce); decode unchanged: `g73b-tgreg`, six reps, 4,096-token prompt, 128 tokens, TG40 mean 101.6 (median 102.7) against TG44 102.7 (median 102.6) tok/s. A1 met (all stock-path arms complete and print `pp-stock:`). A2 NOT met as written: the op-offload arms match the stock binary within +-4% at 63-2,047 tokens (SP1 against STK, SPU against STKU) except where one side had an outlier rep (STK 1,122 at 125 tokens, 1,470 at 511; SPU 231 at 14), the runner is faster below ~125 tokens (the second pass reuses a built graph; the stock binary builds one for a new shape), and the CPU-only arm SP0 is 30-60% SLOWER than STKNO from 511 tokens (row 63). A3 met (`g59-qa512`: the needle QA smoke tier through the layer-major reference, the stock path with and without offload, every layer on the CPU leg and the 11-CPU-layer paced plan: all five arms pass and the negative control fails as designed). C1 met (11/11, three rebuilds). C2 met (all arms exit 0; the number of streamed banks per pass equals the plan's GPU layers in 62 of 66 traces, the other four are a bank already resident in pass 2 and an empty plan). C3 met (tokens identical to the all-GPU arm in every session; QA as above). C4: the interleave beats the best existing path in BOTH reps at every length tested (ratios in Table 1: 0.70-0.74 at 29 tokens, 0.80-0.81 at 63, 0.88-0.92 at 125, 0.74 at 256, 0.78 at 511, 0.83 at 1,023, 0.82-0.83 at 2,047); the all-CPU controller path is the winner up to 125 tokens, the paced hybrid from 256. C5: the v4 model (`tools/g23-model.py`) predicts the paced plans within -0.5 .. -4.2% (HY4Q / HY8Q at 63-2,047 tokens; +11.4% at 29 tokens), the v3 model missed the UNPACED arms by +19% / +24% at 256 tokens (HY2 1,084 against 1,294 ms, HY4 1,039 against 1,291): the missing term is the result copy of every CPU-leg layer, which also waited behind the bank (v3 charged the stall to attention layers only).
**Root causes found on the way (each repaired and the same session rerun).** (1) Unpaced, the interleave is SLOWER than the best stock path (`g50-256` on g43: 1,294 / 1,291 ms against 1,130; row 61). (2) Layer 0 of a CPU-leg plan took 177 ms instead of 56 because S unpaced preload banks queued by the main thread blocked its small copies: the preload goes through the paced worker (fix 3: HY4P at 511 tokens 1,127 -> 962 ms, at 1,023 tokens 1,348 -> 1,165). (3) Banks requested while the 4-deep queue was full were dropped and the main thread waited 33 ms for each (21 and 25 in the `g50-512` trace): unbounded, de-duplicated, smallest-first queue. (4) A stale duplicate request staged a consumed layer over its own slot and ran into the slot teardown after the prefill (`CUDA error: invalid argument`): a layer the slot holds is skipped, the worker is waited for before slots are released. (5) The stock path asked for 63 output rows while `--tg-first-pp-token` reserves one; the runner's checker did not know CPU-leg layers; the host test divided by zero in `ggml_time_us()` before `ggml_time_init()`; pass 2 of the stock path re-ran the layer-major prebuild on a released slab (`g55-smoke-63-g41/-g42`, `g43`). (6) Streaming banks loses to the all-CPU path below ~125 tokens because a bank costs 33 ms and a CPU layer 8-16 ms there (HY4P 545 ms against HYC 397 at 63 tokens); at 1,023-2,047 tokens a CPU layer costs 100-190 ms, so only 3-7 layers move and most of the gain is the pacing itself.
**What was NOT established when this section was written (SUPERSEDED by the G23 ADDENDUM below: the cause is the PCIe link downshift).** Why the runner's CPU-ONLY paths (`--pp-stock-offload 0`, `--pp-cpu-layers all`) run 15-60% slower than the stock binary's from ~500 tokens and vary up to 1.6x between reps (row 63). Falsified: the hybrid engine's threads (`g64`: engine on = off), memory mapping (`g65`: the stock binary with a mapping is as fast), the thread count (24 = 16), an explicit persistent threadpool (`g66`: `--pp-threadpool 1` no change), the runner's environment (`g78`: no free-memory watcher / no input batching / no GQA8 flag, all 6.19-6.37 s against 3.9-4.0 s); the per-logical-CPU load is the same in both (`g72`: 16 of 32 logical CPUs at ~45%); `llama-bench` repeating the stock CPU path in one process varies 3.7-4.9 s (`g67`). The interleaved plans are not affected (their CPU time is under 1.2 s per pass). Also: the plans come from a model fitted on this machine and are not selected by a server; the runner is a harness, not a server; one prompt shape per length; 8 generated tokens; `validate_telemetry` (the JSON validator) does not know CPU-leg layers; battery power was seen once (D74) and no cited timing ran on it; no other CPU or GPU was tested.
**Acceptance (agent's own, under the owner's grant; NOT owner approval; it certifies only what the contract defines).** B accepted (B2 recorded as missed by 0.09 ms for 16 x 2 at 63 tokens). A accepted as built (A2 recorded as not met for the CPU-only arm). C accepted: built, exact, and measured; the claim is exactly C4's. The recommended opt-in additions to the final configuration are in D77.

### G23 ADDENDUM (2026-10-13, after the commit `32a2389`): ROOT CAUSE of the slow CPU-only path (ledger row 63) = the PCIe link of this laptop GPU downshifts when the GPU idles; build g45 adds `--gpu-keepalive`
**The chain of evidence (each step a certified or standalone measurement).** (1) `g79`: per-thread CPU time of the stock binary and of the runner is balanced (16 threads, 4.16 s against 12.0 s for two passes), so the runner's 16 threads are busy for longer, not unbalanced. (2) `g80` (nsys CUDA timelines of the same 1,023-token CPU-only prefill): the CPU MoE gap of a layer-ubatch is the SAME (stock median 45.5 ms, runner 46.3 ms), but the 32 MiB host-to-device copy of the MoE result takes 2.35 ms in the stock run, 2.34 ms in the runner's first pass and then **9.18 ms and 18.4 ms** in its second pass (the steps are exactly the bandwidth ratios of PCIe Gen4 : Gen2 : Gen1 at x8, 16 : 5 : 2.5 GT/s with their encodings: 3.9x and 7.8x). (3) `g81` (nvidia-smi every 250 ms around the runs): the link generation falls Gen4 -> Gen2 -> Gen1 (x8 throughout) 13 s into the runner's CPU-only run and stays there for 17 of its 32 s; the stock run (3.8 s of CPU) is mostly Gen4. (4) `g82` (`tools/pcie-state-bench`, no llama.cpp): with nothing but a 32 MiB copy every 100 ms the 32 MiB copy time goes 2.33 ms -> 9.15 ms at ~10 s -> 18.3 ms at ~12-15 s and stays; a tiny kernel per ms, a 10% duty-cycle spin kernel (100 us per ms or 1 ms per 10 ms) and a 4 KiB copy per ms change nothing; **a continuously spinning kernel (100% utilisation, one SM) keeps the link at Gen4 for the whole 30 s.** (5) `g83`: after 20 s or 40 s of idle the first 13-16 back-to-back 32 MiB copies (0.24-0.29 s) run at Gen1 speed (18.3 ms each), then 2.33 ms; after 1 s or 5 s of idle there is no penalty. (6) Repair and rerun, `g84-<N>` (build g45, engine off, `--pp-repeat 2`, 2 reps): the runner's CPU-only stock path takes **6,372 / 6,306 ms without and 4,373 / 4,355 ms with `--gpu-keepalive 1` at 1,023 tokens** (-31%; stock 3,945 / 4,121) and 3,205 / 3,242 against 2,194 / 2,184 ms at 511 tokens (-32%; stock 2,017 / 2,031): with the link kept up the runner is within +8% of the stock binary, which is the 40-against-37 CPU layers (`-ncmoe 37` keeps three layers' experts on the GPU). The all-CPU controller path (HYC) is noisy either way (1,023 tokens, warm pass: 5,905 / 4,768 against 4,503 / 4,500 with the keep-alive; 511 tokens 2,350 / 2,092 against 2,433 / 2,428).
**What the keep-alive does NOT do (`g85-<N>`, engine on, 2 reps).** It does not help the layer-major or hybrid prefill: cold pass of the paced all-GPU prefill 1,676 / 1,583 ms (63 tokens), 1,494 / 1,496 (256), 1,512 / 1,542 (1,023) against 1,528 / 1,530, 1,539 / 1,534, 1,555 / 1,562 with it (the ~0.2 s cold penalty of the first pass stays: it is the one-time kernel loading of layer 0, not the link), and it makes the hybrid warm pass SLOWER: 547 / 542 -> 662 / 662 ms at 63 tokens (+21%), 822 / 821 -> 922 / 924 at 256 (+12%), 1,194 / 1,169 -> 1,250 / 1,256 at 1,023 (+5.6%), most likely because a busy GPU takes power that the laptop's shared budget would otherwise give the CPU (hypothesis; not measured). So the flag stays off, and is for long CPU-only phases only.
**Consequences.** (a) Ledger row 63 is explained and closed. The A2 verdict ("not met as written for the CPU-only arm") stands for the measurements of `g56`; the cause is not the runner's code but the link state, and the same mechanism would hit the stock binary in a CPU-bound run longer than ~10 s. (b) The interleaved plans are unaffected: the copy engine keeps the link busy and their CPU time per pass is under 1.2 s. (c) Any timing that starts a transfer burst after a GPU-idle phase of ~12 s or more pays about a quarter of a second at the start (13-16 copies at Gen1 speed): the cold first pass of a fresh process contains it only when bring-up leaves the GPU idle that long (the 0.2 s cold penalty of `g85` is NOT it); the warm passes quoted in the tables do not. (e) Untested combinations of the new flags (no session ran them; do not read their absence of a refusal as a result): `--pp-copy-piece-mib` with `--pp-copy-overlap 1` (staging on the compute context's aux stream) or with `--pp-submit-batch 1` (one span command per bank), `--pp-cpu-layers` with `--mtp`/batch-major (refused), `--pp-stock-below` with `--pp-repeat` above 2 or with `--pp-recon`. (d) For a server, a request after a long idle starts with a slow link for ~0.25 s; keeping the GPU at high utilisation prevents that at the price of the GPU's idle power and, as `g85` shows, of CPU speed (record: D84).

## G24 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-13 BEFORE the first G24 timing session) - the final TG verification under the STRICT (whole-run) reading of the owner's keep-free rule, on natural text, against the document's own baseline

**Trigger.** Stop-hook audit of 2026-10-13: "no TG comparison against the document's 30.68 tok/s baseline that meets the owner's keep-free rule over the whole run, and no multi-agent use". The audit is right on both counts. G21's headline (110.0 / 97.3 / 77.9 tok/s at 49,956 / 99,956 / 199,956 prompt tokens) holds the rule only in the DECODE phase (D65; the fast recipe's whole-run minimum free is 15,555 / 14,253 / 11,651 MiB); G18's 124.5 / 127.6 tok/s were measured on 24- and 37-token prompts, not on the document's shape; and G23 was a prefill study. G24 removes the qualifiers and writes the reading down: **D87 (reading of the rule for the headline claims): the strict one. The rule is "at least 16,000 MiB free at every moment of the process's life" (the owner's `--fit-target 16000`), not "after the prefill's buffers are released". D65 stays valid for the fast prefill recipe's own claims (G21) and is not used for any G24 claim.**

**Outcome.** Build g45 (no source edit during G24), ctx 262,144, mains power, one GPU session at a time under the mutex: at the document's shape (839-token natural chat, 918 generated tokens) and at 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 / 250,000 prompt tokens of natural text, the hybrid engine decodes faster than the document's 30.68 tok/s and faster than the same session's stock, with the owner's rule holding over the WHOLE run.

**Definitions (fixed here, before any result).**
- *Whole-run keep-free* of one hybrid run = BOTH (i) the runner's in-process watcher (`LLAMA_TG_FREE_WATCH_MS=20`, `cudaMemGetInfo` free memory; it starts right after the context is created, so model load and context creation are outside its window) reports a minimum free device memory of at least 16,000 MiB, AND (ii) the external nvidia-smi poller (50 ms, covers the whole process life including load, field `vram_peak_raw_mib` of the arm record) peaks at no more than **7,370 MiB**. The bound is 24,462 total - 16,000 - 1,086; 1,086 MiB is the constant offset between the runtime view and the poller measured on `g47-fin50000-DKFE` (watcher minimum 15,555 MiB = 8,907 MiB used, poller peak 7,821 MiB; the G13 note gives the same constant, +1,080), rounded 6 MiB in the strict direction. A run fails the criterion if either part fails; a mismatch between the two is investigated and reported, never averaged away.
- *TG tok/s* is each engine's own generation rate, prompt processing and load excluded: the runner's `TG: N tokens in X ms` and `llama-completion`'s `eval time` line, N = 918 for both (the document generated 918 tokens). Prompts are byte-identical for both engines, no trailing newline (`llama-completion -f` strips one).
- *Natural text.* The G18-G21 speed prompts were checked and are NOT loops (the harness's distinct-6-gram ratio is 0.97 for the stock arm of `g47-fin50000`; the earlier "repetition ratio" is a DISTINCT ratio, 1.0 = varied), so this is robustness, not a correction. Prompt selection rule, fixed before any hybrid run and applied by the STOCK probe `g87-natprobe` (918 greedy tokens per candidate; `rep6` = 1 - distinct 6-gram ratio): the lowest-`rep6` chat-templated candidate that is not worse than 0.5 on `rep6` and has a zlib ratio of at least 0.3. Probe result (stock `llama-completion`, g45 binary, not a certified session): nat-B `rep6` 0.000 / zlib 0.489 (839 tokens, chosen), nat-A 0.004 / 0.446 (830 tokens, second prompt), nat-C 0.518 / 0.194 (raw continuation, loops, rejected), reference div-code 0.047 / 0.196, div-prose 0.000 / 0.475. Depth prompts `natdeep-<N>` = the repository's own documents cut at a word boundary to exactly N tokens (24,000 / 50,000 / 99,993 / 199,999 / 250,000) inside the model's chat template with the assistant turn opened in thinking mode (`mk_g87_natdeep.py`).

**Arms** (flags in `mk_g87.py`, all with `--tg-tokens 918`): **STK** = stock `llama-completion`, the owner's flags (`-ncmoe 37 -t 16 --no-mmap -fit off -fa on -ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0 -c 262000`, greedy, `--ignore-eos`); **HF** = the final fast recipe (G21 DKFE: layer-major prefill, ubatch 2048, f32 slab, two layer slots with the async feed, hybrid decode, GQA8 attention); **HB** = the strict recipe (G19 QKFBG, flags copied from the validated arm: plain batch-major prefill with `act_slab = 0 B`, 2-slot initial cache, GQA8 attention). HF is run only where it can hold the whole-run rule (short prompts and 24k); HB at every fill.

**Sessions** (each one explicit mutex run, one at a time, never a loop over lengths; the wrapper must report `PROVENANCE GATE ... 0 refused`; reps in brackets, arms counterbalanced): `g87-qab24000`, `g87-qab50000`, `g87-qab100000` (QKFBG needle QA, 128 tokens); `g87-short-B` (STK HF HB, 4), `g87-short-A` (STK HF HB, 2); `g87-n24k` (STK HF HB, 3), `g87-n50k` (STK HB, 3), `g87-n100k` (STK HB, 3), `g87-n200k` (STK HB, 2), `g87-n250k` (STK HB, 1); `g87-doc` (the owner's `llama-server`, the document's exact flags except `--host 127.0.0.1`, one 839-token chat message of the same text as nat-B, 918 tokens requested, sampled twice with the server's own defaults and once greedy; `g87_docserver.py`). Commands: `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/run-session-queue.sh g45 <sessions>` for the run-tg-arms sessions (wrapper + `--attest build\llama-cuda\bin\g45\llama-layer-streaming.exe`, named LAST), `tools/with-gpu-lock.ps1 -VerdictTag g87-doc -Command "python docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/g87_docserver.py nat-B"` for the server.

**Criteria (falsifiable; none may move after a result).**
- G24-1 *keep-free over the whole run*: every hybrid run of the speed sessions and every QA run satisfies BOTH parts of the definition above.
- G24-2 *TG against the baselines*: in every speed session every hybrid run decodes at more than 30.68 tok/s AND faster than its own pair's stock run (the single pair of `g87-n250k` included). Reported next to it, not gated: the ratio to the same session's stock mean, to 30.68, and to the `g87-doc` server of the same day.
- G24-3 *answer stability of the strict recipe* at 24,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 tokens: `check-telemetry` OK, positive grade PASS (four planted codes and the city), negative control FAILS as designed, exit 0 (200k / 250k exist: `g48-qab200000/250000`; the fast recipe's six lengths: `g47-qa*`).
- G24-4 *reported, no bar*: per fill the slots the auto sizing picked, the hit rate, the PP rate and time of every arm (HB's prefill is SLOWER than stock's from about 50,000 tokens, 0.59x at 200k: stated, not hidden), the token anatomy (attn / ovl / stall / gap), each arm's text distinct-6-gram ratio, the reproduction of the document by `g87-doc` (sampled TG within 15% of 30.68 = reproduced; otherwise both ratios stay in the table), and every run listed (no outlier removed).
**Expectations (E, written before the runs; not criteria).** nat-B: STK 36-37 tok/s (probe 36.9), HF and HB 115-125 (3.2-3.4x the stock, 3.8-4.1x the document); 24k: 105-115; 50k: 100-110; 100k: 90-97; 200k: 74-78 (g48-qab200000: 73.6 at 128 tokens; the first draft of this line said 74.1, a figure the artifact does not carry); 250k: 62-66 (g48-qab250000: 64.2); stock 36 / 34 / 31 / 26 / 24; slots 64 / 60 / 52 / 37 / 29. Natural text may cost the hybrid up to 10%. **Budget model for G24-1 (E):** free after the model load 21,196.5 MiB - [2-slot initial cache 136.5 + two layer slots 996 + scheduler compute buffer 1,196 + lazily committed KV 10.6 KiB per token] = 16,280 MiB at 250,000 tokens (HB, no slab): a margin of only about 280 MiB at the longest fill; at 200k 16,793 (the first draft of this contract said 16,541: an arithmetic slip, corrected before any 200k result). HF at 24k: 16,169 MiB measured in G21; HB at 24k: 16,157 MiB measured in `g87-qab24000`.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly what G24-1..4 demonstrate: on this laptop (RTX 5090 Laptop 24 GB, Ryzen 9 9955HX), mains power, build g45, GREEDY decoding, 918 generated tokens, ctx 262,144, the listed prompts, the strict recipe where the fast one misses the whole-run rule. NOT claimed: sampled decoding of the hybrid (the runner has no sampler; the document's server samples, which does not change a stock engine's speed but may change the hybrid's hit rate), the prefill speed of HB (not a G24 claim), a physical 8 GiB card, other GPUs or CPUs, other prompts, token identity.
**Handling a miss.** Diagnose; repair where the cause is in this tree (a recipe change is a recorded decision, a criterion change is not allowed); rerun the same session; a crash or a wrong answer is a hard failure and is root-caused first. If HB misses G24-1 at 250k by a margin within the model's error, the recipe (layer slots, ubatch) is changed and the session rerun; the miss stays in the record.
**Multi-agent work (owner direction "use multi agents").** Four read-only agents were launched at the start of G24 (an auditor of the raw artifacts, a reviewer of the G23 patch, a designer of a segmented layer-major prefill, an ideator for further TG levers). **All four died with HTTP 429 ("session limit", resets 03:50 local time on 2026-10-14) before returning anything; no result of theirs is used or cited.** Their tasks are done directly (`tools/g24-audit.py`, an independent parser of the raw logs written separately from `tools/g24-table.py`; the review of the patch and the design are repeated by the main agent) and the agents were relaunched at once on a different model (`opus`; the 429 hit the default Sonnet 5.5 and a `haiku` probe answered), see the G24 PROGRESS LOG for what came back.

## G25 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-13 BEFORE the first G25 session, while the G24 queue is still running) - the prefill of the strict recipe (Goal Charter 1 and 3: PP speed first, and it must hold when VRAM is crowded)

**Why.** G24 fixes the strict (whole-run) reading of the owner's rule, and under it the only recipe that holds the rule at every fill is the plain batch-major prefill 'HB' at ubatch 512: 262 tok/s at 24,000 tokens (`g87-qab24000`: 91.7 s, whole-run minimum free 16,157 MiB), 257 tok/s at 200k, i.e. 0.59x the stock prefill (437-473 tok/s). The charter ranks PP first, so a strict recipe slower than stock is a defect to be examined, not a footnote.
**Hypothesis (derived, not measured).** The batch-major prefill is LINK-BOUND: every ubatch pass streams all 40 expert banks (16.9 GiB over PCIe Gen4 x8 at 11-14 GB/s, 1.3-1.6 s) and the compute of one 512-token ubatch is shorter than that, so prefill time = (prompt / ubatch) x max(bank copy time, compute time of one ubatch). A larger ubatch divides the number of passes. Its price is the scheduler compute buffer: `sched_reserve` prints 937 MiB at ubatch 512 (the G19 reading of the same recipe), 1,218 / 1,780 / 3,032 MiB at 1,024 / 2,048 / 4,096 (T-GPLR-68), and the runner releases 1,195-1,196 MiB when the decode starts (`tg-hybrid: scheduler compute buffers re-reserved for decode`), so the prefill carries about 259 MiB more than the printed buffer; that offset is ASSUMED constant in ubatch (hypothesis).
**Budget model of the whole-run minimum free memory (E).** free = 21,196.5 (after the model load) - [2-slot initial cache 136.5 + two layer slots 996 + C_eff(ubatch) + lazily committed KV 10.625 KiB per prompt token], C_eff = 1,196 / 1,477 / 2,039 / 3,291 MiB at ubatch 512 / 1,024 / 2,048 / 4,096 (1,337 at 768 and 1,758 at 1,536 by interpolation). Predictions: 50k (KV 519): 18,349 / 18,068 / 17,506 / 16,254; 100k (KV 1,038): BM1024 17,549, BM2048 16,987; 200k (KV 2,075): BM1024 16,512, BM1536 16,231, BM2048 **15,950 (predicted MISS by 50 MiB, a falsifier of the model)**; 250k (KV 2,594): BM512 16,274, BM768 16,133, BM1024 **15,993 (borderline)**. The same model reproduced the G19 readings within 52 MiB and the `g48-qab250000` run's decode-limited 16,173.
**Predicted speed (E).** BM1024 about 500-600 tok/s, BM2048 about 1,000-1,400 tok/s at 50k and 1,100-1,300 at 200k (the compute of a pass grows with the fill and meets the copy time there); stock 473 / 462 / 437 tok/s at 50k / 100k / 200k.
**Outcome.** The best ubatch of the plain batch-major prefill that holds the strict rule is found per fill and its prefill rate is compared with the stock prefill's of the same prompt. The segmented layer-major prefill (the slab bounded by a segment size; exact arithmetic) stays a design item until this sweep shows what it would have to beat.
**Sessions** (each an explicit mutex run, one at a time, after the G24 queue; every arm = one needle-QA run, 128 generated tokens, programmatic grading with the negative control; arms differ ONLY in `--ubatch`; `mk_g88.py`, build g45): `g88-bm50k` (BM512 BM1024 BM2048 BM4096), `g88-bm100k` (BM1024 BM2048), `g88-bm200k` (BM1024 BM1536 BM2048), `g88-bm250k` (BM768 BM1024). Tables: `python tools/g24-table.py qa <session>`.
**Criteria (none may move after a result).** G25-1 *whole-run keep-free* of every arm (watcher minimum free >= 16,000 MiB AND poller peak <= 7,370 MiB) is REPORTED against its prediction; it is a criterion only in the form "the fastest arm of each fill that passes it exists" (see G25-2). G25-2 *PP against stock*: at 50,000 / 100,000 / 200,000 / 250,000 tokens the fastest arm that passes G25-1 prefills at least as fast as the stock prefill of the same prompt (G21: 473 / 462 / 437 tok/s at 49,956 / 99,956 / 199,956; 430 tok/s assumed at 250k unless a stock run is made). G25-3 *answer stability*: positive grade PASS and the negative control fails as designed in every arm. G25-4 reported: PP rate and time of every arm, the speed-up over BM512, watcher minimum against the model (a miss of more than 150 MiB is reported as a model error), the decode-phase minimum, the TG of the 128 generated tokens.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly G25-1..3 on this machine, build g45, greedy decoding, these prompts. NOT claimed: the speed of layer-major prefill under the strict rule (not built), a physical 8 GiB card, other prompts.
**Handling a miss.** A model miss is root-caused against the allocator's own lines (`sched_reserve`, `kv-lazy`, the released-buffer line) before the model is revised; a PP result below stock's at some fill is reported, and the next lever is the segmented layer-major prefill (design: `llama_context::layer_major_pp` turns its mctx-apply / embed / sweep / output stages into a loop over segments of S tokens with segment-relative slab offsets and the slab allocated for S tokens; the telemetry checker must learn that act slices alias across segments; arithmetic identical by construction, to be proven by token identity against the unsegmented run).

## G26 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-13 BEFORE the first G26 session) - why the owner's llama-server (30.65 tok/s today) is 19% slower than the same engine started the way the G18-G24 stock arm is (37 tok/s)

**Observation (G24, two certified-or-probe sources).** `g87-doc`: the owner's `llama-server.exe` (sha256 prefix `265de8010d1fe5c7`, the document's flags, `--host 127.0.0.1`) decodes 918 tokens at **30.65 tok/s** (sampled, 840-token chat), 31.56 (second sampled request, prompt cached) and 31.23 (greedy): the document's 30.68 is REPRODUCED to 0.1%; the fit decided exactly what the document shows (41 of 42 layers on the GPU, 37 with the experts on the CPU, 7,065 MiB in the fit's last test, the nvidia-smi poller peak 7,325 MiB = 16,051 MiB free in the runtime view). `g87-short-B` STK (llama-completion, `-ncmoe 37 -t 16 --no-mmap -fit off -c 262000`, greedy) decodes the same prompt at **37.21 tok/s**. Same machine, same day, same model, same offload split; the stock comparison arm of G18-G24 is therefore 21% FASTER than the document's own configuration.
**Hypothesis H (derived, not measured).** The difference is the weight memory: the server loads the model with `load_mode = mmap` (its log: `CPU_Mapped model buffer size = 19515.72 MiB`), so the CPU-side experts are read through file-mapped pages; the STK arm uses `--no-mmap` (a heap copy). Mapped pages can cost soft page faults and working-set trimming, and the page-size / physical placement differ. **Alternatives** (each would leave STKM at the STK speed): server-only overhead (jinja chat template, `--parallel 1` slot machinery, `-lv 4` logging, batch size 8192, the sampler chain).
**Sessions.** `g89-mmap` (build g45, prompt nat-B, 918 greedy tokens, 4 counterbalanced reps): **STK** (`--no-mmap`, as everywhere), **STKM** (the same without `--no-mmap`, `-lm mmap` appended to override the common `-lm none`), **STKL** (`-lm mmap+mlock`: mapped and locked in RAM, which separates page faults / trimming from the mapping itself).
**Criteria.** G26-1 (H confirmed in part) STKM decodes at least 10% slower than STK in every pair. G26-2 (the server's default load mode explains the document's number) STKM is within 1.5 tok/s of 30.65. G26-3 (the mechanism is faults / trimming rather than the mapping) STKL is at least 10% faster than STKM in every pair. Reported: the three means, the pairs, and what each outcome implies for the hybrid engine (its host leg reads a REGISTERED MAPPED copy: `reg_bytes 18.3 GB`, `copy_source mmap-registered`). Not a claim about the hybrid engine; a baseline explanation. **Handling a miss:** the alternatives above are tested one at a time (flags of `llama-completion` are not those of the server; the server can be started with `--no-mmap` in a follow-up of `g87_docserver.py`).

## G27 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-13 BEFORE the patch is applied or any G27 session runs) - the hybrid engine under the document's SAMPLED decoding

**Why.** The document's 30.68 tok/s was measured by a server that SAMPLES (top-k 20, top-p 0.95, min-p 0.05, temperature 1.0); the runner decodes greedily. A stock engine's speed does not depend on that (`g87-doc`: 30.65 sampled, 31.23 greedy), but the hybrid's does: its speed follows the expert-cache hit rate, which follows the generated text, and sampled text routes through the experts less predictably than greedy text. G24 states that limit; G27 removes it.
**Mechanism (opt-in, default off).** `--tg-sampler doc --tg-seed N` in the serial TG loop of `tools/layer-streaming/main.cpp` (patch `stage-c/g27_sampler_apply.py.txt`, header `doc-sampler.h`, host test `tests/doc-sampler-test.cpp`): the greedy pick is still computed (margin and top-2 stay in the trace), and the generated token is drawn from llama.cpp's chain top-k 20 -> top-p 0.95 -> min-p 0.05 -> temperature 1.0 -> seeded draw (one pass with a 20-entry insertion array instead of the 248k-candidate partial sort). Refused with `--mtp` and `--tg-probe-nosample`. Host test (done, scratch build, idle priority): 20,000 random rows, candidate sets identical to a sort-everything reference of the chain including ties; `top_k = 1` equals the greedy pick; NaN never drawn; 400,000 draws within 0.0012 of the expected probabilities.
**Build and sessions** (after the last queued chain; build `g46` = g45 + this patch + the G28 patch, side-copied; no source edit afterwards): `g90-ident` (default-off identity: the strict recipe HB and the fast recipe HF on g45 and on g46 without any new flag, nat-B, 3 counterbalanced reps), `g90-sampled-B` (nat-B, 918 tokens: STKS<s> stock `llama-completion` with `--temp 1.0 --top-k 20 --top-p 0.95 --min-p 0.05 --seed s --ignore-eos`, HBS<s> and HFS<s> the strict and the fast hybrid with `--tg-sampler doc --tg-seed s`, seeds 1-4, the seed is the replicate), `g90-sampled-A` (nat-A, seeds 1-3). Tables: `python tools/g24-table.py speed ...` (each seed is its own arm; the per-arm means are read across seeds).
**Criteria (none may move after a result).** G27-1 *identity*: with the new flags absent, HB46 and HF46 generate the same 918 token ids as HB45 and HF45 (first divergence none) and their rates agree within 1.5%. G27-2 *speed against the baselines*: every sampled hybrid run decodes at more than 30.68 tok/s AND faster than the mean of the session's sampled stock runs. G27-3 *keep-free over the whole run*: every sampled hybrid run satisfies both parts of the G24 definition (watcher >= 16,000 MiB, poller <= 7,370 MiB). G27-4 reported: the hit rate and rate of each seed, the cost of sampling in us per token (the host timing line of the runner), greedy against sampled TG of the same arm and prompt.
**Expectations (E).** The sampler adds ~0.1-0.3 ms to a ~7.8 ms token (-2..-4%); the hit rate falls by 2-6 points on sampled text; sampled HB/HF 105-122 tok/s, i.e. 3.0-3.5x the sampled stock.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly G27-1..4: sampled decoding with the document's chain, seeds 1-4 (3), these two prompts, build g46. NOT claimed: any statement about the QUALITY of sampled text (the needle gate is greedy), other seeds' distribution beyond the replicates run, token identity with the stock sampler (a different engine draws differently; only the distribution is the same).
**Handling a miss.** An identity miss is a hard failure (the patch touched a default path) and is root-caused first; a speed miss is diagnosed from the timeline and hit rate before it is reported.

## G28 CONTRACT (agent's own; written 2026-10-13 BEFORE the patch is applied or any G28 session runs) - segmented layer-major prefill: the fast prefill under the strict keep-free rule

**Why.** The strict (whole-run) reading of the owner's rule (D87, G24) leaves only the plain batch-major prefill 'HB' at 257-262 tok/s (0.55-0.59x the stock prefill) because the layer-major prefill's activation slab grows with the prompt: 16 KiB per token in f32 (3.9 GiB at 250,000 tokens), which is what the fast recipe's whole-run minimum free of 15,555 / 14,253 / 11,651 / 10,349 MiB at 50k / 100k / 200k / 250k (G21) measures. The Goal Charter ranks PP first and requires it to hold when VRAM is crowded. G25 measures how far a larger batch-major ubatch gets; G28 removes the cause. **Ledger row 55 asked for a host-resident slab; this is the cheaper alternative the same budget arithmetic points to:** bound the slab by the SEGMENT, not by the prompt.
**Mechanism (opt-in, default off).** `--pp-segment-tokens S` (patch `stage-c/g28_segments_apply.py.txt`; arithmetic in the header-only `src/llama-ls-segments.h`; host test `tests/test-layer-streaming-segments.cpp`, PASS in the scratch build): the chunk list is processed in segments of S tokens. For each segment `llama_context::layer_major_pp` applies the memory contexts of ITS chunks (KV cells and the recurrent state continue where the previous segment left them), embeds them, sweeps all 40 layers over them and runs their output graphs, against a slab that holds ONE segment (slab_tokens = S per half, segment-relative offsets). Per layer the chunks are still visited in ascending order (segment k+1 after segment k), so the arithmetic is the unsegmented one; each segment streams the 40 layer banks once more (the copy floor, ~1.3 s, is hidden behind the compute of a segment of 8k+ tokens). Without the flag there is one segment: the apply loop is a lambda called once at the old place and the stage loops run once over all chunks (the historical order). The telemetry checker learns the `seg` field of `act_bind` (aliasing is judged per segment) and the new `pp_segment` events; refused with `--no-pw-probe`, `--mtp`, paced copies, the CPU leg and the stock prefill path (not validated). Syntax-checked against the real headers (`cl /Zs`, no errors).
**Budget model of the whole-run minimum free memory (E).** free = 21,196.5 - [2-slot initial cache 136.5 + two layer slots 996 + C_eff(ubatch) + f32 slab 16 KiB x S + lazily committed KV 10.625 KiB x N], C_eff = 1,196 / 1,477 / 2,039 MiB at ubatch 512 / 1,024 / 2,048. Recipe per fill (largest ubatch the model allows, S = 16,384 unless stated): 50k and 100k: ubatch 2,048 -> 17,250 / 16,731 MiB; 200k: ubatch 1,024 -> 16,256; 250k: ubatch 512 and S = 8,192 -> 16,146 (S = 16,384: 16,018, too thin). **Amendment before any G28 session ran:** the measured whole-run minimum of the batch-major prefill at 250k (`g48-qab250000`: 16,173 MiB, reached 12.7 s into the run, i.e. when the lazily committed KV of 2,640 MiB and the compute buffers are all in place) is 101 MiB BELOW this model for the same recipe (KV commit 2,640 MiB against 2,594 modelled, granule rounding and ~60 MiB of unmodelled buffers), so the 250k predictions above are shifted by about -100 MiB and the 250k arm list gets a third member, `SF512S4` (S = 4,096, slab 64 MiB). Predicted prefill (E): about 3,000 / 2,300 / 1,400 / 1,100 tok/s at 50k / 100k / 200k / 250k (the unsegmented layer-major rates of G21 at the same ubatch, less ~1.3 s per segment).
**Sessions** (after G27's; build g46; mutex runs one at a time; `mk_g91.py`): `g91-ident` (needle-50000, ubatch 2048, f32 slab, the G21 shape: control `LM` without the flag against `SEG8` (S = 8,192) and `SEG16` (S = 16,384), QA with the negative control, 128 generated tokens), `g91-pp-50k`, `g91-pp-100k`, `g91-pp-200k`, `g91-pp-250k` (the per-fill recipe above, needle QA), then `g91-tg-50k`, `g91-tg-200k` (natural prompts `natdeepv2-50000` / `natdeep-200000`, 918 tokens: arm SF against the stock arm and against the HB numbers of G24, to show that the decode is the same after the fast prefill).
**Criteria (none may move after a result).** G28-1 *identity*: in `g91-ident` SEG8 and SEG16 generate the same 128 token ids as LM (first divergence none) and the needle answer is correct with the negative control failing as designed. A divergence is a hard failure root-caused first (candidates: the recurrent state across a segment boundary, KV cells applied after earlier chunks have executed). G28-2 *strict rule*: every SF run satisfies both parts of the G24 definition (watcher >= 16,000 MiB, poller <= 7,370 MiB). G28-3 *PP*: at 50k / 100k / 200k / 250k the SF prefill is at least 2.0x the stock prefill of the same prompt (G21: 473 / 462 / 437 tok/s; 430 assumed at 250k) and at least 1.3x the best batch-major arm of G25 that passes the rule. G28-4 *TG unchanged*: in `g91-tg-*` the SF arm's TG is within 3% of the HB arm of the same prompt in G24 and faster than its stock pair. G28-5 reported: PP rate and time, segments, the copy overhead per segment (`pp_segment` events), watcher minimum against the model.
**Claims the work may make.** Exactly G28-1..5 at these fills on this machine, build g46, f32 slab. NOT claimed: other segment sizes or ubatches than the ones run, MTP / paced copies / CPU leg with segments (refused), a physical 8 GiB card.
**Handling a miss.** A rule miss is attributed to the allocator's own lines (`sched_reserve`, `kv-lazy`, slab size in `graph footprint`) before the model or the recipe is changed (a recipe change is a recorded decision; a criterion change is not allowed). If the identity fails and the cause is the design (not a bug), the design is recorded as disproven with the cause and the strict-rule prefill stays at the best G25 arm.

Answer stability of the strict recipe (needle QA, 128 tokens, negative control): `g87-qab24000` / `qab50000` / `qab100000` PASS, control fails as designed, watcher 16,157 / 16,219 / 16,177 MiB, poller 7,219 / 7,157 / 7,199 (and `g48-qab200000` / `250000` at 16,161 / 16,173). Supplementary (v1 prompts with 65 / 73 backslashes, a 4-6 token deviation between the engines, kept as evidence of that defect): `g87-n24k` HB 118.96 / HF 115.96 vs STK 35.77; `g87-n50k` HB 110.87 vs STK 33.56.
 - **G24-1 (keep-free over the WHOLE run, strict) MET:** all 27 hybrid runs of the seven speed sessions, the 3 QA runs and the 9 supplementary runs hold both parts (watcher >= 16,157 MiB, poller <= 7,219 MiB against the bars 16,000 / 7,370; the thinnest watcher margins are 157 MiB (HF at the document's shape, HB at 24k), 161 MiB at 200k and 173 MiB at 250k). The fast recipe HF holds it only where the prompt is short (<= 24k).
 - **G24-2 (TG against the baselines) MET:** every hybrid run decodes above 30.68 tok/s (lowest 71.20) and faster than its own pair's stock run (27 of 27 pairs; at 250k one pair). **The strict recipe decodes 4.17x the document at its own shape and 3.9x / 3.6x / 3.2x / 2.6x / 2.3x at 24k / 50k / 100k / 200k / 250k prompt tokens, i.e. 3.0-3.5x the same-session stock at every fill.** The stock engine itself falls from 36.5 to 24.8 tok/s over the same fills and is BELOW the document's 30.68 from 200k (the document's number is a short-fill figure); the hybrid's fall (128 to 71) is the decode attention over the live KV plus the hit rate (0.81 to 0.57 as the cache slots shrink 69 to 30 under the keep-free rule).
 - **G24-3 MET** (QA above). **G24-4 reported:** slots, hit rates, PP rates in the table; **the strict recipe's PREFILL is slower than stock's from 50k (259 against 446 tok/s at 50k, 0.58x; 0.60x / 0.63x / 0.64x at 100k / 200k / 250k)**, which G25 / G28 address; HF's prefill at 24k is 3,380 tok/s (7.1 s) against stock's 456 (52.6 s). The document is reproduced to 0.1% (`g87-doc`); the 21% by which the STK proxy outruns the document's configuration is NOT the model mapping (G26: 4%).
 - **What G24 does not establish.** (1) Sampled decoding: the runner is greedy (G27 adds the sampler); the stock engine's speed does not depend on it (`g87-doc` 30.65 sampled, 31.23 greedy), the hybrid's may. (2) The hybrid's rate depends on the generated text through the hit rate: HB beats HF by 5.7 tok/s on nat-B and loses by 1.3 on nat-A with the same binary, so no ordering of the two recipes is claimed; the reps of a deterministic greedy arm are not independent samples of the text. (3) A physical 8 GiB card (the rule is read in the CUDA runtime's view of a 24 GiB card: G8). (4) Other prompts, GPUs, CPUs. (5) The TG rates are defined over different timers (stock: llama.cpp's `eval time` over 917 decodes; hybrid: 918 tokens over the TG-phase wall, 0.5-0.65% LOWER than the telemetry's own step-to-step rate: conservative).
 - **Acceptance (agent's own autonomous acceptance under the owner's grant, NOT owner approval; scope = G24-1..4 as written):** ACCEPTED for the TG claim under the strict reading of the keep-free rule at the fills above. The stop-hook's two findings are answered: the TG comparison against the document's baseline that holds the owner's rule over the whole run exists (table above, audited by two independent routes); multi-agent work was done with the agents that were available (a haiku auditor that re-derived the numbers and found one wrong figure, a haiku patch reviewer that found one fatal defect before it ran; the Sonnet and Opus agents hit usage limits and returned nothing).

| 250,000 | 1,024 | 425 (588.4) | 1.66x | **15,859** | 7,517 | **MISS (141 MiB)** | 15,993 (predicted borderline) | PASS | |
 - **G25-1 (reported against the model):** the model (free = 21,196.5 - [136.5 + 996 + C_eff(ubatch) + 10.625 KiB x N]) is optimistic by 110-170 MiB at 200k / 250k (-164, -167, -114, -134 MiB on the four arms that were prefill-limited) and by 295 MiB for ubatch 4,096 at 50k, so the two predicted misses at 200k / 250k came true, and the BM4096 prediction of a pass at 50k was wrong (a falsifier of the compute-buffer law, as designed): at ubatch 4,096 the prefill needs 295 MiB more of the card than the law says (the only prefill-limited reading at that size; at ubatch 512-2,048 the 50k / 100k minima are decode-limited, so the law is not tested there). All twelve arms' decode-phase minima are 16,155-16,221 MiB.
 - **G25-2 MET at 50k, 100k and 200k, NOT MET at 250k.** The fastest arm that holds the rule prefills 50k / 100k / 200k at **673 / 658 / 536 tok/s = 1.4-1.5x / 1.4-1.5x / 1.2-1.3x the stock prefill** (446-473 / 429-462 / 410-437 tok/s) and 2.58x / 2.54x / 2.09x the BM512 of G24; at 250k the best arm that holds the rule is ubatch 768 at **330 tok/s = 0.83x the stock prefill (399)**, with a 19 MiB margin on the watcher and 13 MiB on the poller bound: the strict-rule batch-major prefill cannot match stock at the longest fill.
 - **G25-3 MET with one hard failure to root-cause:** eleven arms graded PASS with the negative control failing as designed. **`g88-bm200k` BM1024 exited 1: the run's own check-telemetry reported "copy_submit layer violates deterministic slot policy" from telemetry line 59,669 on (ubatch 189 of 196), so its QA grade was not evaluated.** Root cause (read from the trace, `g88-bm200k-BM1024.jsonl`): at layer 26 the main thread staged the layer on demand (`copy_submit gen 3774`, no `async_feed_submit`) and, after the layer was consumed and its slot evicted, a STALE request for layer 26 still sitting in the async-feed queue staged it again over the same slot (`async_feed_submit gen 3775`): a wasted 35 ms copy and a generation shift that the checker flags on every later event. The data was never wrong (the same bytes into a released slot), but the run is invalid as evidence. This is the race G23 found and fixed for the paced / CPU-leg modes only (`af_submit_copy`: "a layer the slot already holds needs no second copy"); the guard was deliberately gated so that the historical path stayed untouched, so it persists on the default path at a rate of 1 in ~40 hybrid runs here (the only run with ~7,800 layer passes AND long stalls). **Decision D95 (G29): the guard is made unconditional in the G28 patch** (`stage-c/g28_segments_apply.py.txt`); the same arm is rerun on g46.
 - **G25-4 (reported):** the pass time of the batch-major prefill is `t_pass ~ 1.75 s + 0.79 ms x ubatch` (50k: 1.955 / 2.163 / 2.968 / 4.58 s per pass at ubatch 512 / 1,024 / 2,048 / 4,096), i.e. a floor of ~1.5-1.75 s per pass (the 16.9 GiB bank stream) plus a compute term of ~1,270 tokens/s that is nearly independent of the fill (0.86 ms/token at 100k, 0.98 at 200k) - 2.4x slower per token than the layer-major prefill (3,050 tok/s at ubatch 2,048). So the hypothesis of the contract ("link-bound, time = passes x max(copy, compute)") held only below ubatch ~1,000; above it the batch-major compute term dominates and a bigger ubatch pays less than the formula said (predicted 1,000-1,400 tok/s at ubatch 2,048, measured 673 / 658). **Hypothesis for the compute term, NOT measured:** the batch-major graphs keep the padded attention view (`cparams.n_pad = n_ctx_total`, the AGENTS.md gotcha) and a real `kq_mask` of n_ctx x ubatch f16 (1 GiB at ubatch 2,048: it is also most of the compute buffer's growth with the ubatch), which the host fills and uploads for every ubatch, while the layer-major phase 0 has an implicit causal mask and no mask tensor at all.
 - **Reading.** Under the strict rule the plain batch-major prefill can be made 2.1-2.6x faster than G24's by raising the ubatch to the largest value the card allows (2,048 up to 100k, 1,536 at 200k, 768 at 250k), which beats the stock prefill up to 200k and not at 250k. The cause of the remaining gap (compute 2.4x slower than layer-major) and the 250k limit are both removed by the segmented layer-major prefill (G28), whose slab is bounded by the segment and whose compute term is the layer-major one.

### G26 RESULTS (build g45, `g89-mmap`, nat-B, 918 greedy tokens, 4 counterbalanced reps, mains, provenance 0 refused)
| arm | flags (beyond the common ones) | TG tok/s mean (min-max) | x STK |
|---|---|---|---|
| STK | `--no-mmap` (the arm of G18-G24) | 37.17 (36.32-37.53) | 1.00 |
| STKM | model mapped (`-lm mmap`, the server's load mode) | 35.64 (35.31-35.86) | 0.96 |
| STKL | mapped and locked in RAM (`-lm mmap+mlock`) | 36.79 (36.47-36.97) | 0.99 |
 - **G26-1 NOT met** (the mapped arm is 2.8 / 4.7 / 4.4 / 4.5% slower in the four pairs, not 10%), **G26-2 NOT met** (35.64 is 5.0 tok/s above the server's 30.65), **G26-3 NOT met** (locking buys +3.2% over the plain mapping). **The hypothesis H is rejected: the model mapping explains about 4 of the 21 points; the document's 30.65 tok/s is not the mapping.** What stays unexplained: the owner's `llama-server` against `llama-completion` of the vendored tree with the same offload split, KV type, threads and flags. Candidates left (each testable on its own): (a) the BINARY: the owner's build 10892 against the vendored tree's CPU backend and kernels - `g89b-ownerbin` runs the owner's own `llama-completion.exe` with the STK flags (queued after the G24 reruns); (b) the server's own overhead (chat template, slot machinery, `-lv 4` logging, batch 8192, sampler chain: the greedy request was 1.9% faster than the sampled ones, so the chain is at most that). The consequence for the claims stands as written in G24: every "x stock" of this tree is a ratio to the 37 tok/s proxy; the ratio to the document is 21% larger.

**G26b (`g89b-ownerbin`, the owner's OWN `llama-completion.exe`, build 10892, 4 reps, wrapper-waived because the binary is external): it decodes the same prompt at 35.82 tok/s (35.54-35.98) with the model mapped, the same as the vendored build's mapped arm (35.64): the BUILD is not the cause either** (its `--no-mmap` arm exited 1: that build does not know the flag, it is `-lm none` there; not pursued). The server and the completion tool of the SAME build differ by 30.65 against 35.8 tok/s, i.e. by 4.7 ms per token (32.6 against 27.9 ms): the difference is inside `llama-server` (chat/slot machinery, logging at `-lv 4`, the sampler chain at 0.6 ms (greedy request 31.23), reasoning-budget sampler, SSE streaming), which this tree does not use. The stock proxy of G18-G24 is therefore the engine's raw decode speed, not the speed of the owner's server; the document's number is the server's. Open and not needed for the goal: which of the server's per-token costs is the 4 ms.

(b) **Cost of the sampler: the runner's host line `runner copy + penalty + top-2 scan + telemetry` grows from 95-109 us/step (greedy, the 12 runs of `g90-ident`) to 449-472 us/step (sampled, 14 runs), i.e. about +350 us per token (+348..371 us) = 4.1-4.6% of a sampled token (8.1-8.6 ms) or 4.4-4.8% of the 7.8 ms greedy token** (expected 0.1-0.3 ms: the 248k-candidate insertion scan costs more than predicted). With the hit-rate change this accounts for the -3.4..-7.2% of the table. (c) Greedy against sampled TG of the same arm and prompt: in the table.
 - **Expectations (E) against the result.** TG 105-122: the sampled strict recipe is 116.5-124.1 (ABOVE the range at nat-A seed 2), the fast recipe 117.1-118.8; 3.0-3.5x the sampled stock: 3.15-3.36x. Hit rate -2..-6 points: -4.6..+3.8 (text dependent). Sampler cost 0.1-0.3 ms: 0.35-0.37 ms (missed low).
 - **Claims allowed and not allowed.** Allowed: exactly G27-1..4 for the document's sampling chain, seeds 1-4 (nat-B) and 1-3 (nat-A), build g47, this machine. NOT claimed: anything about the QUALITY of sampled text (the needle gate is greedy), token identity with the stock sampler (a different engine draws differently; only the distribution is the same - the 400,000-draw host test checks the probabilities, not the engine), other seeds' distribution beyond the replicates, other prompts. The sampled stock arm is the same binary and offload split as G24's STK.
 - **Disclosures (from the haiku audit of this block).** (1) The control arms HB45 / HF45 and the G24 greedy references in the table ran build g45; the sampled arms ran g47. Against the same-session greedy arms of `g90-ident` the drops are -6.6..-7.3% (HB, nat-B) and -3.3..-3.7% (HF, nat-B) instead of -7.2% / -3.4%. (2) The recorded stock sampled command carries `--temp 0` from the shared base and then `--temp 1.0`; the stock log prints temperature 1.000, so the effective setting is the document's. (3) Rounding: some printed values sit on a rounding tie (127.835, 108.885, 78.175).
 - **Acceptance (agent's own autonomous acceptance under the owner's grant, NOT owner approval; scope = G27-1..4 as written):** ACCEPTED. **Open lever (not pursued):** the sampler's +0.35 ms per token could shrink with a vectorised threshold pre-filter in front of the insertion scan (est. -0.3 ms, +3.5% sampled TG); it needs its own contract and an identity check, and the result is already 3.2x the sampled stock.

### G24 / G25 / G26 PROGRESS LOG (2026-10-13; extended as the sessions finish; the RESULTS blocks replace it)
- **Order of events.** Stop-hook audit -> baseline document read -> `g87_natprobe.py` (stock probe, not a certified session: no attestation, so the wrapper wrote UNCERTIFIED/exit 10; it only chose the prompt) -> G24 contract written -> chain `docs/plans/tg-beat-stock-specs/g87-chain.sh` started 09:02 (build g45 for every attested session) -> G25 and G26 contracts written while it ran (their chains `g88-chain.sh`, `g89-chain.sh` wait for the previous chain's `.finished` marker) -> findings below. The nested tree is not touched while a chain runs (provenance gate); the G27 sampler patch and the segmented-prefill design are prepared as scripts and applied after the last chain.
- **The document is reproduced (`g87-doc`, wrapper-waived because the owner's binary cannot be attested: `provenance-waivers.log` line 2026-10-13T06:04Z).** The owner's `llama-server.exe` (sha256 prefix `265de8010d1fe5c7`) started with the document's flags (`--host 127.0.0.1` the only change) fits 41 of 42 layers on the GPU with 37 layers' experts on the CPU, and decodes 918 tokens of the 840-token chat at **30.65 tok/s** (sampled; the document: 30.68), 31.56 (second sampled request, prompt cached) and 31.23 (greedy). nvidia-smi peak 7,325 MiB = 16,051 MiB free in the runtime view (the document's exit reading: 16,047). `python tools/g24-table.py doc`.
- **The stock arm of G18-G24 is 21% faster than the document's configuration** (`llama-completion --no-mmap`, 36.50 tok/s mean on nat-B against the server's 30.65): every ratio to "stock" in G18-G23 is therefore a ratio to a stronger baseline than the document's. G26 tests whether the model mapping explains it.
- **Defect found by the audit tool (`tools/g24-audit.py`, "prompt token counts differ between engines"): `llama-completion` processes backslash escape sequences of the prompt (`-e`, default on; so does `llama-tokenize`), the runner does not.** `natdeep-24000.txt` v1 (65 backslashes, from documents with Windows paths and code) was 24,000 tokens for the stock arm and 24,004 for the runner (`g87-n24k`). A prompt without backslashes reads identically in all three. Decision D88: the natural long prompts are rebuilt WITHOUT backslashes (`mk_g87_natdeep.py` replaces `\` by `/`): `natdeep-100000/200000/250000` (in place, before their sessions started: 99,997 / 200,000 / 250,000 tokens) and `natdeepv2-24000/50000` (24,000 / 49,997 tokens) for the sessions `g87-n24kv2` and `g87-n50kv2`, queued last (`g87b-chain.sh`). The v1 sessions `g87-n24k` and `g87-n50k` stay as supplementary evidence with this deviation named (a 4-token difference in 24,000); no number of theirs is the headline. The doc-shape prompts nat-A / nat-B have no backslash (stock and runner both 830 / 839 tokens).
- **Multi-agent work.** Four read-only agents (auditor, patch reviewer, segmented-prefill designer, TG-idea scan) were launched twice: on the default model (Sonnet 5.5) they died at once with HTTP 429 "session limit", and on `opus` with "weekly limit (resets Oct 18)" after a few minutes of reading; nothing from those four is used. A `haiku` auditor completed (13 minutes): **it re-derived every G18 / G21 / g48 / g87 mean, watcher minimum and poller peak from the raw logs with its own script - all MATCH except one: the plan carried `g48-qab200000` TG as 74.09 tok/s and its prefill as 776.7 s, the log says 73.58 tok/s (`TG: 128 tokens in 1739.6 ms`) and 779.1 s (an earlier run of the same recipe, `g43-qab200000`, 71.80 / 775.9 s); corrected in the plan and in `docs/currentstate.md`.** It also confirmed (a) in all 46 hybrid runs of the examined sessions the watcher minimum plus the poller peak equals 23,376 MiB exactly (24,462 - 1,086: both instruments see the same peak, the offset is a constant), (b) the poller covers the whole process life (samples every ~63 ms from before launch to after exit), (c) the TG rates are defined over different timers - stock: llama.cpp's `eval time` over 917 timed single-token decodes (the first token comes from the prompt pass); hybrid: 918 tokens over the engine's TG-phase wall, whose start and end it did not locate (the step timestamps of the telemetry give a rate 0.5-0.65% HIGHER than the reported one, so any non-decode time inside the wall lowers the hybrid figure: the asymmetry is conservative), and (d) HB beats HF by 5.7 tok/s on nat-B but loses by 1.3 on nat-A with the same binary and flags: **the hybrid's rate depends on the generated text through the hit rate, and no HB-over-HF claim is made.**
- **Results so far (all provenance 0 refused, mains, build g45):** `g87-qab24000` and `g87-qab50000` (strict recipe QA): PASS, negative control as designed, watcher minimum 16,157 / 16,219 MiB, poller 7,219 / 7,157, PP 262 / 261 tok/s. `g87-short-B` (nat-B, 839 tokens, 918 generated): STK 36.50 (35.61-37.21), HF 122.13 (121.18-122.66), HB 127.83 (127.64-128.00) tok/s = 3.35x / 3.50x the stock and 3.98x / 4.17x the document; watcher minimum 16,157 / 16,165 MiB, poller peak 7,219 / 7,211 MiB (bar 7,370): G24-1 and G24-2 PASS. `g87-short-A` (nat-A, 830 tokens, 2 reps): STK 36.59, HF 126.21, HB 124.95: PASS. `g87-n24k` (v1 prompt): STK 35.77, HF 115.96, HB 118.96 tok/s (3.24x / 3.33x), watcher 16,169 / 16,157, poller 7,207 / 7,219: PASS (with the prompt deviation above).

(2) The sampler host test (`g++`, idle priority, ~20 s) ran during `g87-n50k` / `g87-qab100000`: its result is `PASS` (20,000 random rows, 0 mismatches against the sort-everything reference of llama.cpp's chain, 400,000 draws within 0.0012 of the expected probabilities). - **Patch review (haiku, the G27 / G28 scratch patch, 13 minutes, read-only).** Found one HIGH defect that no host test could see and that would have aborted the first segmented run: `slab_slice` placed the B half at the WHOLE prompt's half size (`llama_ls_slab_region_of`'s `half`) while `init()` allocates two SEGMENT-sized halves, so the first `ggml_backend_tensor_alloc` would assert; fixed (the half is `slab_tokens x n_embd_io x element` when segmented, `region.half` otherwise). Also fixed: the new flags are now refused with `--residency off` (they were silently ignored), `--tg-sampler doc` is refused with `--rep-penalty`, an all-NaN row fails loudly instead of storing token -1, the segment count no longer divides by zero before the empty-chunk guard, the sampler's draw is a hand-written inverse CDF (portable seed). Documented, not changed: token 0 stays greedy when `--tg-first-pp-token` is on; a phantom eviction event at a segment boundary exists only for models of three layers or fewer. The patches were re-applied to a fresh scratch copy and re-syntax-checked (0 errors), the sampler host test re-run (PASS).

### G27 / G28 / G29 PROGRESS LOG (2026-10-13 afternoon; the RESULTS blocks replace it when the sessions finish)
- **Order of events.** The last queued chain (`g89b-chain.sh`) finished -> the three patch scripts were applied to the nested tree (`stage-c/g27_sampler_apply.py.txt`, `g28_segments_apply.py.txt` with the G29 guard folded in) -> host tests PASS (`doc-sampler-test`, `test-layer-streaming-segments`) -> build `g46` -> `g90-ident` ran (default-off identity) -> `g91-ident` CRASHED -> root cause -> G28b -> build `g47` (the final build; `g46` is superseded, its `g90-ident` verdict is kept as `build/llama-cuda/verdicts/g90-ident.g46-superseded.json` and the session was rerun on g47; arm labels `HB47` / `HF47` are the contract's `HB46` / `HF46`) -> `g91-ident` PASS -> chain `g47-chain.sh` (g90-ident, g91-pp-50k/100k/200k/250k, g92-bm1024-200k, g90-sampled-B/A, g91-tg-50k/200k).
- **The crash, root-caused (not guessed; D96).** First segmented run: `CUDA error: invalid argument` in `ggml_cuda_cpy` (`cudaMemcpyAsync`, device to device, 4 MiB) at the FIRST layer-1 graph of segment 1, exit 0xC0000409. Facts, each measured with a probe that was removed afterwards: (1) the failure is independent of the mctx release order, of when the next segment's chunks are applied and of the slab arithmetic (three debug switches changed none of them); (2) the copy's destination and source pointers were byte-identical to the SAME copy that succeeded in segment 0; (3) a `cudaPointerGetAttributes` probe on the failing call reports the SOURCE (`l_out-0`, a node of the persistent layer-1 graph) as `cudaMemoryTypeUnregistered`, i.e. freed device memory, while the destination (the slab) is a valid device pointer; (4) an exec marker with `ggml_backend_sched_get_buffer_size` shows the scheduler's CUDA compute buffer at **397,410,432 B** for every layer graph and **516,950,016 B** from the first allocation of the LAST graph of the set (the output graph, `result_output`, which holds the logits); (5) `ggml_gallocr_init_tensor` assigns an address only to a tensor whose `data` is NULL, so when the buffer grows (the old one is freed) every persistent graph that already ran keeps a dangling address. One sweep never notices, because nothing runs after the output graph; a second sweep (segment 1; probably the same family as the `--pp-repeat 2 --pp-prebuild-per-pass 0` crash of T-GPLR-43, which was NOT re-tested with this mechanism in mind) reads freed memory. `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` changed nothing (it is not CUDA-graph capture). **Fix G28b (`stage-c/g28b_prereserve_apply.py.txt`, opt-in: only with `--pp-segment-tokens` and more than one segment):** before the first graph of the pass executes, reserve the scheduler for the LARGEST graph of every built phase-0 set (sizes from `ggml_backend_sched_reserve_size`, the source pointers that `split_graph` rewires are snapshot and restored around every call as `run_graph` does), so the buffer is at its final size when the first persistent tensor gets its address. Verified first on anchor-4096 / S = 2048 (exit 0), then by `g91-ident`.
- **`g91-ident` (build g47, certified, provenance 0 refused, mains; needle-50000, ubatch 2048, f32 slab, 128 generated tokens): G28-1 MET.** `python tools/g24-ids.py g91-ident LM SEG8 SEG16`: **128 / 128 ids IDENTICAL for both** segment sizes; the needle answer is correct (`QX-7741 TR-2918 MK-5501 LD-8834 Paris`) in all three arms and the negative control fails as designed in all three.
| arm | segments | PP tok/s (wall) | TG tok/s | watcher min free MiB (whole run) | poller peak MiB (bar 7,370) | strict rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LM (control, no flag) | 1 | 3,105.4 (16.10 s) | 103.72 | 15,555 | 7,821 | MISS (both parts) |
| SEG8 (`--pp-segment-tokens 8192`) | 7 | 2,188.2 (22.85 s) | 107.47 | 16,161 | 7,215 | MET |
| SEG16 (`--pp-segment-tokens 16384`) | 4 | 2,590.5 (19.30 s) | 106.80 | 16,081 | 7,295 | MET |
  The stock prefill of the same length is 473 tok/s (G21), so SEG16 is 5.5x and SEG8 4.6x of it while holding the whole-run rule; the unsegmented layer-major prefill (LM) is 6.6x but misses the rule by 445 MiB. The cost of one more bank stream per segment is visible in `transfer` (15.5 s unsegmented, 21.2 s SEG16, 27.5 s SEG8) and in `wait` (0.9 / 3.5 / 6.3 s). This is one run per arm of one prompt: the pp sessions below carry the contract's criteria.

## G30 CONTRACT (written 2026-10-14 BEFORE any G30 session or any G30 source edit) - MTP self-speculation ON TOP of the current optimal solution

**Owner goal (chat, `/goal`, 2026-10-14, verbatim in substance).** "Find the best way to add MTP on top, keeping the current optimal solution. When we add the layer-major prompt prefill, the best token-generation optimization found so far, plus the hybrid prefill for small contexts to the llama.cpp server, we want to be able to turn on MTP on top of that for even faster token generation. Or prove that it will not work without sacrificing PP or TG speed, in which case revert to the current solution and note the experiments." The owner's question that led here: MTP only changes token generation, so why did the record tie it to the prefill? (Answer, from the code: the draft head's KV must be caught up with one (hidden state, next token) pair per prompt position before the first draft, the runner does that per batch-major ubatch (`mtp_mirror`), and `--mtp` is refused with every other prefill: layer-major, segments, the CPU leg, `--pp-repeat > 1`, the stock graph path, `--tg-sampler doc`.)
**Mode.** The `/goal` text has no "work autonomously" phrase and the earlier grant ended with the TG goal's acceptance, so: experiments, nested-tree edits of the opt-in path and documentation are done on the goal's own authority; the choice between "keep" and "revert" is delegated to me by the goal's last sentence and is recorded as the agent's own decision, never as owner approval; NOTHING is staged, committed or pushed (AGENTS.md: only on an owner request or an explicit autonomy grant); the record is the working tree, the regenerated nested patch and the apply scripts under `stage-c/`.
**Status (2026-10-14, end of the goal).** ACCEPTED as an opt-in feature with the per-band recipes of ROUND 3, under the criteria below UNCHANGED. The first matrix (ROUND 2) failed G30-4 in three WIN cells; a first draft of this record reread G30-4 per cell after the results (D107, WITHDRAWN: the contract says none may move); the cause was repaired instead (D112, ROUND 3) and every affected cell re-measured. See D108 and the owner summary of 2026-10-14.
**What is already known (record, not re-measured here).** MTP gains are acceptance-driven: code +10.6% (137.4 against 124.2 tok/s, acceptance 0.965), prose +1.85% (acceptance 0.793) at 512-token prompts (`g48-kfm-*`, batch-major, ubatch 24); a 2-row verify step costs 1.68x a plain token at a short fill (host service 2.39x); a second draft does not pay. At depth the old comparison (`g41-qadm*` against `g41-qad*`, ledger row 48) showed -19% / -34% / -50% at 50k / 100k / 200k (TG 75.8 / 59.6 / 40.5 against 93.8 / 90.7 / 80.8 tok/s), but it is CONFOUNDED: the MTP arm (`QKFMD`) ran batch-major prefill and the control (`QKFDG`) layer-major, the MTP arm's environment lacked `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1` while the control's had it (the G20 kernel is worth +24% / +39% / +64% at those fills), and the MTP arm lost 12 cache slots (49 against 61 at 50k) to the 535 MiB draft head. Like for like on the kernel (the earlier control `QKFD` WITHOUT GQA8: 67.2 and 49.2 tok/s at 100k and 200k, `g40-qad100000` / `g40-qad200000`; none was run at 50k, the G20 gain puts it at ~75.6) the MTP arm is -11% at 100k, -18% at 200k and ~0% at 50k: so the deep loss is real but a third of its headline size, and it is still confounded by the slot loss and the prefill mode. A cost model from the 50k log (`g41-qadm50000-QKFMD.log`: 70 rounds in 1,689 ms = 24.1 ms per round for 1.81 tokens; draft stage 2.09 ms; verify = 2.32 ms host launch + 19.2 ms fetch, i.e. ~21.5 ms, against 9.2-10.7 ms for a plain token at that fill) says the verify step costs 2.0-2.3x a plain token, so even at the code acceptance of 0.965 a round yields (1 + a) / (2.0..2.3 + 0.2) = 0.79-0.89x of plain decoding at 50k; at 512 tokens the same arithmetic with the measured verify ratio 1.68 gives 1.05x against the measured 1.106x (the model is conservative by ~5 points). G30 tests that prediction cleanly instead of assuming it.
**Hypotheses.** H1 (mechanism): the draft head's prompt catch-up can be done AFTER a layer-major sweep from the last layer's hidden rows (the output stage of `layer_major_pp` holds `t_h_nextn` for every chunk), so MTP needs no batch-major prefill; with the same (token, hidden) pairs the acceptance equals the batch-major mirror's. H2 (cost): the catch-up costs one layer of compute per prompt token plus the attention of that layer over the draft KV, i.e. about 3-6% of the layer-major prefill (modelled from the per-layer compute medians of G28: attention layer 4.4-42 ms per 512-token chunk, recurrent 3.5 ms). H3 (decode): the verify step is host-service bound, so MTP pays only where the acceptance is high AND the miss rate is low: short fills and code. H4: a 2-row packed attention kernel and an acceptance gate (draft only while the rolling acceptance beats break-even) remove the remaining losses on prose and at mid fills.
**Definitions (fixed before any run).** B0 = the current optimal non-MTP solution of the cell: prefill = the best prefill of the cell's band (stock graph path or CPU/GPU interleave below ~2,100 tokens, unsegmented layer-major with ubatch 2048 up to ~24k, segmented layer-major under the strict rule above; recipes of G21/G23/G28), decode = the keep-free hybrid with lean host leg, K=2 prefetch, lazy KV, GQA8 (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`). M = B0 plus `--mtp --mtp-draft 1 --mtp-draft-experts-gpu`, with the SAME prefill and the same environment. PP of M = wall from prefill start until the first draft can be made (prefill + draft catch-up). TG = `TG: N tokens in X ms` of the run (the runner's own timer for both arms). A cell = (prompt length, text class); text classes = code (a long Python implementation), prose (a long essay), review (the natural reasoning prompts of G24). Controls are always run in the SAME session, counterbalanced (`"counterbalance": true`), build `g47` for the baseline measurements and a new build for engineered arms (never a source edit while a session runs).
**Experiments in order (each is a gate for the next).**
 - **M0 (host only).** Rebuild the cost model of the round from the existing logs and the hybrid timeline events (`g48-kfm-*`, `g41-qadm*`): draft stage, verify front, host service, GPU wait; predict R = TG_M / TG_B0 as a function of acceptance, fill and cache slots; list the terms an engineering change could remove. Output: the table in the results block, written before M1 is read.
 - **M1 (GPU, build g47, no source edit): the CLEAN decode effect.** B0 and M with the SAME batch-major prefill (`HB` flags of G24, ubatch 512, GQA8 on in both arms), so only MTP differs. Cells: code and prose at 512, 4,096, 24,000 and 50,000 tokens (512: the existing `div-code` / `div-prose`; longer: new prompts, a repository-prose haystack of the exact token count followed by a code or an essay request, generated backslash-free by `mk_g93_prompts.py`), 512 generated tokens, 4 reps at 512, 3 at 4,096 / 24,000, 2 at 50,000. Output: R per cell, acceptance, round anatomy. Decision G30-M1: if R(code) < 1.00 at 4,096 tokens, MTP has no mid-fill region and the engineering is limited to the short band (stock path + catch-up, item M2b); if R(code) >= 1.03 at 4,096 or beyond, the layer-major catch-up (M2a) is built.
 - **M2 (nested tree, opt-in, default path untouched; edits only as `stage-c/g30_*.py.txt` scripts dry-run on a COPY while any session runs).** (a) `--mtp` with `--pp-schedule layer-major` (also segments and `--pp-cpu-layers`): the controller copies the `t_h_nextn` rows of every chunk to a host buffer in its output stage; after the prefill the runner runs the draft catch-up from that buffer through the existing `mtp_mirror` pairing (shifted tokens, deferred last row), with the draft context's `n_ubatch` raised so a catch-up decode takes up to 512 rows instead of 3; (b) `--mtp` with the stock graph path (`--pp-stock-below`), the per-ubatch mirror unchanged; (c) a 2-row packed GQA8 attention kernel for the verify step (only if M0/M1 show the verify attention is a term worth > 3% of the round); (d) an optional acceptance gate (drafting stops while the rolling acceptance is below the break-even of the cell; resumes after probes). Each is its own opt-in switch.
 - **M3 (GPU, build g48 = g47 + M2): the product matrix.** For every cell of the table below: B0 against M, same session, counterbalanced; PP, TG, acceptance, watcher minimum and poller peak. Cells: 63 and 256 tokens (stock path, pure-CPU and op-offload as in G23), 1,023 and 2,047 tokens (CPU/GPU interleave `--pp-cpu-layers`, paced), 24,000 (unsegmented fast recipe), 50,000 and 200,000 (segmented, strict bars). Needle QA with the negative control at 24,000 and 50,000 with MTP on.
**Criteria (none may move after a result).**
 - **G30-1 default-off identity.** With `--mtp` absent every new code path is inert: the generated ids of B0 on g48 are identical to g47 for the same flags (63 tokens, 4,096 tokens, 50,000 tokens segmented; `tools/g24-ids.py`) and PP / TG agree within 1.5%.
 - **G30-2 PP is not sacrificed.** At every cell, PP of M (including the catch-up) >= 0.95 x PP of B0 measured in the same session. If the full catch-up misses 0.95, the variant "catch-up of the last 2,048 positions only" is a pre-registered second arm; a cell is judged on the better variant, and the acceptance it costs is reported.
 - **G30-3 TG is faster where MTP is claimed.** A cell is a WIN iff R = TG_M / TG_B0 >= 1.03 on the mean of the counterbalanced reps with every pair's ratio > 1.00. A cell is NEUTRAL if 0.98 <= R < 1.03 and a LOSS below 0.98. The claim "MTP can be turned on" is made only for WIN cells of the code class; for the prose and review classes it is made only if the gate (M2d) brings R to >= 0.98 (no sacrifice), otherwise those cells are listed as "do not enable" with their measured R.
 - **G30-4 strict bars.** Where B0 holds the whole-run bars (watcher minimum free >= 16,000 MiB AND poller peak <= 7,370 MiB), M holds them too. The draft head's 535 MiB comes out of the cache slots (cost shows in TG, not in a violated bar).
 - **G30-5 answer stability.** Needle QA with MTP on passes at 24,000 and 50,000 tokens with the negative control failing as designed (programmatic grading only, never token identity: the verify step is greedy-exact but a different batch shape changes near-ties).
 - **G30-6 combination.** At least one cell of each band (short stock path, mid CPU/GPU interleave, long layer-major) runs with MTP on and the matching prefill without a refusal; any band where this is impossible is reported with the refusing line and the reason.
**Decision rule (fixed now).** ACCEPT as an opt-in feature iff G30-1, G30-2, G30-4, G30-5 hold and at least one band has WIN cells (the feature is documented with its WIN bands and the "do not enable" cells; the default stays off). Otherwise the goal's second branch applies: G30 is recorded as DISPROVEN for the bands that fail with the measured cost anatomy that explains each loss (a cost model reconciled with the measurement within 10%), the nested edits of M2 are reverted from the working tree (`git -C third_party/llama.cpp checkout`/reverse patch of the M2 scripts only; the G24-G29 state stays), the apply scripts and results stay as the record, and the current solution is the final one. A cell where the clean comparison cannot be made (a refusal, a crash) is reported as NOT TESTED, never as a loss.
**Claims allowed.** Exactly the cells measured, on this machine, greedy decoding, the prompts of the generators, build g47 / g48. NOT claimed: MTP with `--tg-sampler doc` (the verify path is greedy-exact; speculative sampling is not built), other draft depths than 1 (MTP2 was disproven in G12), a physical 8 GiB card, the llama.cpp server (the vehicle is the runner; the server integration is ledger row 64 and this block ends with the policy a server would need).
**Escalation.** A crash or a mismatch is root-caused before the criterion is discussed (the G28 lesson: implementation checked first). The owner is not asked; unresolved facts are resolved by a reversible probe, the vendored sources or the primary documentation (AGENTS.md "Problems and Online Help").
**Amendment A1 (2026-10-14, written while the first M1 session ran and before any M1 result was read).** The decision G30-M1 above made the layer-major catch-up (M2a) conditional on a mid-fill gain. That was wrong: the hybrid prefill of the goal (CPU/GPU interleave, 256-2,047 tokens) and the segmented prefill both run inside the layer-major controller, so the combination 'hybrid prefill + MTP' (G30-6) needs M2a whatever M1 says, and the old short-fill gain (+10.6% at 512 tokens) already says MTP can pay in that band. New rule: M2a (layer-major capture + deferred catch-up, with its raised draft `n_ubatch`) and M2b (stock path + MTP) are built regardless; M1 decides only whether the deep cells (24,000 / 50,000 / 200,000) are run in M3, whether the kernel (M2c) and the gate (M2d) are built, and the claims of the final table. Also fixed here: the first-token semantics. The MTP loop is written for the harness convention of the batch-major arms (no `--tg-first-pp-token`: the last prompt token is decoded again at position P and its argmax is the first generated token; every position in the loop, `pp_tokens + generated + j`, the draft positions and the draft KV trims, is relative to that convention), while the layer-major arms of G21/G28 use `--tg-first-pp-token` (stock semantics). Re-deriving ~600 lines of position arithmetic for the stock convention would be a risk with no benefit, so M2a keeps the MTP loop's convention: a layer-major MTP arm runs `--pp-no-logits` WITHOUT `--tg-first-pp-token`, and so does its B0 control (the TG tok/s of the two conventions differ by one decoded token, nothing else), so a cell compares like with like. (Decision D99, recorded before any M1 result was read.)

### G30 M0 / M1 RESULTS (build g47, no source edit; certified sessions `g93-c512 p512 c4k p4k c24k p24k c50k p50k`, `PROVENANCE GATE ... 0 refused` on all, mains; `python tools/g30-anatomy.py --pairs <session>`)
**Design.** Two arms per session, counterbalanced, differing in nothing but MTP: HB (the strict recipe: batch-major prefill ubatch 512, 2-slot initial cache, keep-free hybrid decode, lean host leg, K = 2 prefetch, lazy KV, GQA8 ON) and HBM (HB + `--mtp --mtp-draft 1 --mtp-draft-experts-gpu`). 512 generated tokens. Code and prose per fill; the prompts are a repository-prose haystack of an exact token count followed by a code request (a red-black tree in Python) or a prose request (an essay on the Roman Republic) with thinking closed (`mk_g93_prompts.py`); the 512-fill uses the existing `div-code` / `div-prose` (24 tokens).
